And she makes a beautiful turkey, always brines it for two days with apple cider and peppercorns... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm just in this light sleep, and every little creak in the house wakes me up, and then I'm just lying there until way before the alarm goes off, and I just never feel rested.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely tired or sleepy during the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, constantly. It's like I'm dragging myself through molasses all day. By the time I finish seeing my home-health clients—and Mr. Henderson is just one of them, I also have Mrs. Gable who lives in this third-floor walk-up, and let me tell you, carrying her groceries up those stairs is a workout. She always wants these specific canned peaches, the ones in heavy syrup, not the juice. I told her the juice is better for her blood sugar, but she says she's 82 and she'll eat what she wants. Which, I guess, fair enough, right? My aunt Edna was the same way with her peppermint candies. She kept them in this little crystal dish on the coffee table, the kind that looks like a swan. I always loved that dish. I think my cousin Barbara ended up with it after the estate sale, which is typical Barbara, she always swooped in and took the best things... \n\nBut anyway, by the time I get back to my own house to help out, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing the simplest things, like charting my notes for the agency or just folding a basket of laundry, it feels like it's just... too much to push through. I'm just so drained.\n\nDoctor: Is your mother-in-law's presence the main thing you associate with your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say she's the only reason I don't sleep, but it definitely all started when she moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago now. We had to convert the den into a bedroom for her, which was a whole ordeal because my husband had all his woodworking tools in there. He makes these beautiful birdhouses, you know? Really intricate ones that look like little Victorian mansions. He sells them at the craft fair down at the Methodist church every spring. The pastor's wife bought one last year and painted it this awful neon pink color. I mean, who paints a Victorian birdhouse neon pink? It completely ruined the natural grain of the cedar. Cedar is so expensive right now, too, with all the supply chain issues. We went to Home Depot the other day and a single piece of plywood was almost double what it used to be. The guy working in the lumber aisle said it's because of the shipping containers getting stuck... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, my mother-in-law. Yeah, it's been about two years since she moved in, and that's exactly when my sleep just went completely out the window.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a regular sleep schedule?\n\nPatient: I try to, but it's really hard with my job. I usually try to get to bed around 10:30, but it really depends on when my husband gets home from his second job. He works security at the mall on the weekends and a couple of weeknights. It's the mall over on Route 9, the one that used to have that huge Sears? They tore it down and put in a giant gym with one of those indoor pools. I don't know who has time to go swimming in the middle of the day, honestly. My friend Linda goes to water aerobics there on Tuesdays. She says it's great for her knees, but she always smells like chlorine for two days afterward. I can't stand the smell of chlorine, it reminds me of when my kids were little and we'd go to the municipal pool, and the lifeguards were always blowing their whistles at everything... \n\nSo, yeah, I don't really have a set time, I guess. It just depends on the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up with a start or a sense of panic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a panic, really, but my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. I'll fall into bed completely exhausted, but then I just wake up to every little sound in the house. Like the pipes clanking. My husband tried to fix them last month with this antique pipe wrench he found at a garage sale over on Elm Street. The guy running the sale had all these vintage tools, and this huge box of old vinyl records. I used to love listening to records when I was taking a few classes at the community college, I had this Fleetwood Mac album I played until it literally wore out. You just don't get that same warm sound with digital music nowadays, you know? My kids just stream everything on their phones with those little wireless earbuds. I tried a pair once and they just kept falling right out of my ears, I don't understand how people run in those things without losing them down a storm drain... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I hear every creak and groan of the house, and then I'm just awake way before my alarm even goes off. I always get up feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually take naps during the day to try and feel more rested?\n\nPatient: I try not to, but sometimes I just can't help it. I'll sit down on the couch after I finish my shift—I work as a home health aide, mostly with elderly folks in the county. Some of them are just so sweet, like Mr. Henderson. He always offers me hard hard candies from this little crystal dish he keeps on his coffee table. It's one of those heavy, cut-glass bowls that you just don't see anymore. He said it belonged to his grandmother, who brought it over from Ireland. I've always wanted to go to Ireland, actually. I saw this documentary on PBS once about the Cliffs of Moher, and the green is just so vibrant, it almost looks fake. I tried to grow some shamrocks in a pot on my windowsill for St. Patrick's Day a few years ago, but my cat, Mittens—she's a tabby, a real menace sometimes—she just chewed them all down to the dirt. She does that to all my plants. I had to put my spider plant in a hanging basket just to keep it safe from her...\n\nBut, um, naps. Right. I don't really take them on purpose. I just kind of nod off on the couch sometimes, but it doesn't really help. I still feel just as tired when I wake up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling most awake and alert at the same time every day, regardless of when you go to bed or wake up?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. Honestly, I don't think I ever feel fully awake or alert, no matter what time it is. I'm just always kind of dragging. I try drinking coffee, but I can only have one cup in the morning or my stomach gets all upset. My husband bought me one of those fancy espresso machines for my birthday last year, the kind with the little pods? It takes up half the counter space in our kitchen. Our kitchen is so small anyway, it's one of those galley kitchens from the 70s with the original yellow linoleum floor. I've been wanting to rip that floor up for years. I saw this beautiful slate tile at the home improvement store last weekend when I was looking for a new showerhead. The one we have now just trickles out water, it's so frustrating when you're trying to rinse the shampoo out of your hair. I use this special shampoo for color-treated hair, because my grays are just coming in so fast lately. I tried dyeing it myself out of a box a few months ago and it turned out this awful brassy orange color, I had to wear a hat for a week until I could get an appointment at the salon...\n\nSo, no, I don't really have a time where I feel awake. I'm just tired all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself falling asleep unintentionally during the day, such as while sitting or talking to someone?\n\nPatient: While talking to someone? Oh, no, I don't think I've ever done that. I mean, it would be pretty rude, wouldn't it? Though, to be honest, sometimes people talk so much you kind of wish you could just close your eyes and tune them out. Like my sister-in-law, Brenda. She called me last night and just went on and on for almost an hour about her new dog. It's a golden retriever mix she got from a rescue down in Springfield. Springfield is about a two-hour drive from here, and the traffic on the interstate is just horrendous this time of year with all the construction. They've been working on that same overpass since last summer, and they always close down the left lane right when everyone is trying to get home from work. My husband got a flat tire in that construction zone a few months ago because of a loose nail. We had to wait for the tow truck for over an hour in the freezing cold. He was so mad, because he had just bought those tires at Costco. Costco is great for things like that, though I usually just go there for the bulk paper towels and those big tubs of mixed nuts. They have the best macadamia nuts, I like to bake them into white chocolate chip cookies when the holidays roll around... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, falling asleep. No, I don't just fall asleep in the middle of a conversation or sitting around during the day. Just that nodding off on the couch after my shift that I mentioned earlier.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself typically waking up at the same time every day, even on weekends or days off?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I don't have a set time or anything. It kind of just depends on the day. On my days off, I try to sleep in a little, but the garbage trucks always come by so early on Saturdays. They make this awful screeching noise when they lift the bins. Our neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out there in her bathrobe once to yell at them because they dropped some trash on her driveway. She's very particular about her driveway. She just had it repaved last summer by this company that her son works for. He’s a nice boy, but he drives this massive pickup truck that’s so loud, it rattles our front windows when he comes to visit her. I’ve been meaning to get those windows replaced anyway, they let in such a terrible draft in the winter. We put that plastic shrink-wrap stuff over them last November, and it took me forever to get the tape residue off the wood frames in the spring. I had to use almost a whole bottle of Goo Gone. That stuff smells exactly like oranges, doesn't it? It reminds me of the orange marmalade my grandmother used to make. She'd put it in these little glass jars with the checkered lids and give them out for Christmas... \n\nWait, what were you asking? Oh, waking up. Yeah, no, it's never the exact same time. I just wake up whenever.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No, my hours are pretty normal. I'm a home health aide, so I just work regular daytime shifts mostly. Right now I'm mainly with this one client, Mrs. Higgins. She's a dear, really, but she keeps her thermostat set to 78 degrees year-round. It is just stifling in there. The sleep... well, this has really been going on since my mother-in-law moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago. We had to move her in because she couldn't manage the stairs at her old place anymore, so we converted the downstairs den into a bedroom for her. \n\nMy husband insisted we repaint it first because she absolutely hates the color green—she says it reminds her of a hospital ward. So we spent an entire weekend painting it this peach color. I think the paint can called it 'Sunset Glow' or something ridiculous like that. But the smell of the paint just lingered for days, getting into all the furniture. I had to wash the living room curtains three times just to get the fumes out, and even then, every time the baseboard heater kicked on, you could still smell it. Plus she brought this old Persian cat with her, and the hair just gets into everything, no matter how much you vacuum...\n\nDoctor: Did your sleep start to get worse after your mother-in-law moved in with you?\n\nPatient: Well, I really couldn't tell you if it was exactly then that the sleep started getting so bad. It's hard to pin down the exact timeline, to be honest. But speaking of that cat, she insists on feeding it this incredibly smelly canned food. Not the normal stuff you get at the grocery store, but this special brand you have to order online. It smells like low tide at the beach, I swear. \n\nMy husband—bless him, he tries to help—he accidentally ordered the chicken flavor instead of the salmon last month, and you would have thought the world was ending. She actually made him drive to three different pet stores on a Sunday just to find the salmon one. And Sunday is his only day to just sit and watch football, which is a whole other argument in our house because he yells at the TV when the referee makes a bad call, and it gets the dog next door barking. The neighbors actually left a passive-aggressive note on our mailbox about it once. It was written on this floral stationery that smelled like cheap lavender perfume... so, anyway, I'm really not sure if my sleep got worse right at that specific time she moved in. It all just sort of blurs together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the stress of having your mother-in-law live with you is affecting your sleep?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe? It's stressful, sure, but... I don't know if that's exactly what's waking me up. But speaking of stress, you wouldn't believe what happened with her laundry last week. She has these delicate little embroidered handkerchiefs—who even uses handkerchiefs anymore, right? But she insists they have to be washed separately on the delicate cycle with this specific, unscented baby detergent. \n\nWell, the washing machine has been acting up. It makes this horrible grinding noise during the spin cycle, like metal scraping on metal. My husband said he'd look at it, but he's been so busy at the hardware store. They're doing inventory this month, and he comes home smelling like sawdust and industrial cleaner. He tracked mud all over the hallway runner on Tuesday, and I just bought that runner at a home goods store. It has this lovely geometric pattern, but mud just sinks right into the fibers. I spent an hour scrubbing it with club soda, which my grandmother always said was the best for stains... so, yeah, I'm not really sure if it's the stress of her being there that's messing with my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Is there a particular time of day or night when you most often wake up?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not really one specific time. My sleep has just been completely broken for a long time now. I fall into bed absolutely exhausted, but then I wake up to every single little sound in the house. \n\nLike the ice maker in the fridge, for instance. We got this new refrigerator last Black Friday because the old one kept leaking water all over the kitchen linoleum. And let me tell you, getting someone to come out and fix linoleum is impossible these days. The handyman wanted three hundred dollars just to look at it! Can you believe that? My neighbor, Susan, she had her whole kitchen redone a few years ago for less than what these people charge for a patch job now. She went with this gorgeous subway tile, though I told her white grout is an absolute nightmare to keep clean, especially if you cook a lot of pasta sauce, which she does because her husband is Italian and his mother gave her all these old family recipes... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the sleep. Every little sound wakes me up. And then I'm just lying there, and I end up wide awake way before my alarm even goes off, feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you typically wake up feeling rested before the noises wake you up, or are you waking up from a light sleep already?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think I'm ever really in a deep sleep to begin with, honestly. Like I was saying, it's just broken. I fall into bed absolutely exhausted after being on my feet all day at work. Being a home health aide is no joke on the lower back, let me tell you. My client, Mr. Henderson, he's a sweetheart, but he refuses to use his walker when he goes to the bathroom. He insists he can hold onto the walls, but he's already fallen twice this year. \n\nThe last time he fell, the EMTs had to come, and one of them was this young kid who looked like he was barely out of high school. He had this little mustache he was trying to grow, you know the kind? It just looks like dirt on their upper lip. My nephew tried to grow one of those when he went off to college, and my sister was absolutely mortified when he came home for Thanksgiving looking like a 1970s game show host. She made him shave it right there in the downstairs half-bath before she'd even serve the turkey. And she makes a beautiful turkey, always brines it for two days with apple cider and peppercorns... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm just in this light sleep, and every little creak in the house wakes me up, and then I'm just lying there until way before the alarm goes off, and I just never feel rested.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely tired or sleepy during the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, constantly. It's like I'm dragging myself through molasses all day. By the time I finish seeing my home-health clients—and Mr. Henderson is just one of them, I also have Mrs. Gable who lives in this third-floor walk-up, and let me tell you, carrying her groceries up those stairs is a workout. She always wants these specific canned peaches, the ones in heavy syrup, not the juice. I told her the juice is better for her blood sugar, but she says she's 82 and she'll eat what she wants. Which, I guess, fair enough, right? My aunt Edna was the same way with her peppermint candies. She kept them in this little crystal dish on the coffee table, the kind that looks like a swan. I always loved that dish. I think my cousin Barbara ended up with it after the estate sale, which is typical Barbara, she always swooped in and took the best things... \n\nBut anyway, by the time I get back to my own house to help out, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing the simplest things, like charting my notes for the agency or just folding a basket of laundry, it feels like it's just... too much to push through. I'm just so drained.\n\nDoctor: Is your mother-in-law's presence the main thing you associate with your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say she's the only reason I don't sleep, but it definitely all started when she moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago now. We had to convert the den into a bedroom for her, which was a whole ordeal because my husband had all his woodworking tools in there. He makes these beautiful birdhouses, you know? Really intricate ones that look like little Victorian mansions. He sells them at the craft fair down at the Methodist church every spring. The pastor's wife bought one last year and painted it this awful neon pink color. I mean, who paints a Victorian birdhouse neon pink? It completely ruined the natural grain of the cedar. Cedar is so expensive right now, too, with all the supply chain issues. We went to Home Depot the other day and a single piece of plywood was almost double what it used to be. The guy working in the lumber aisle said it's because of the shipping containers getting stuck... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, my mother-in-law. Yeah, it's been about two years since she moved in, and that's exactly when my sleep just went completely out the window.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a regular sleep schedule?\n\nPatient: I try to, but it's really hard with my job. I usually try to get to bed around 10:30, but it really depends on when my husband gets home from his second job. He works security at the mall on the weekends and a couple of weeknights. It's the mall over on Route 9, the one that used to have that huge Sears? They tore it down and put in a giant gym with one of those indoor pools. I don't know who has time to go swimming in the middle of the day, honestly. My friend Linda goes to water aerobics there on Tuesdays. She says it's great for her knees, but she always smells like chlorine for two days afterward. I can't stand the smell of chlorine, it reminds me of when my kids were little and we'd go to the municipal pool, and the lifeguards were always blowing their whistles at everything... \n\nSo, yeah, I don't really have a set time, I guess. It just depends on the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up with a start or a sense of panic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a panic, really, but my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. I'll fall into bed completely exhausted, but then I just wake up to every little sound in the house. Like the pipes clanking. My husband tried to fix them last month with this antique pipe wrench he found at a garage sale over on Elm Street. The guy running the sale had all these vintage tools, and this huge box of old vinyl records. I used to love listening to records when I was taking a few classes at the community college, I had this Fleetwood Mac album I played until it literally wore out. You just don't get that same warm sound with digital music nowadays, you know? My kids just stream everything on their phones with those little wireless earbuds. I tried a pair once and they just kept falling right out of my ears, I don't understand how people run in those things without losing them down a storm drain... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I hear every creak and groan of the house, and then I'm just awake way before my alarm even goes off. I always get up feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually take naps during the day to try and feel more rested?\n\nPatient: I try not to, but sometimes I just can't help it. I'll sit down on the couch after I finish my shift—I work as a home health aide, mostly with elderly folks in the county. Some of them are just so sweet, like Mr. Henderson. He always offers me hard hard candies from this little crystal dish he keeps on his coffee table. It's one of those heavy, cut-glass bowls that you just don't see anymore. He said it belonged to his grandmother, who brought it over from Ireland. I've always wanted to go to Ireland, actually. I saw this documentary on PBS once about the Cliffs of Moher, and the green is just so vibrant, it almost looks fake. I tried to grow some shamrocks in a pot on my windowsill for St. Patrick's Day a few years ago, but my cat, Mittens—she's a tabby, a real menace sometimes—she just chewed them all down to the dirt. She does that to all my plants. I had to put my spider plant in a hanging basket just to keep it safe from her...\n\nBut, um, naps. Right. I don't really take them on purpose. I just kind of nod off on the couch sometimes, but it doesn't really help. I still feel just as tired when I wake up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling most awake and alert at the same time every day, regardless of when you go to bed or wake up?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. Honestly, I don't think I ever feel fully awake or alert, no matter what time it is. I'm just always kind of dragging. I try drinking coffee, but I can only have one cup in the morning or my stomach gets all upset. My husband bought me one of those fancy espresso machines for my birthday last year, the kind with the little pods? It takes up half the counter space in our kitchen. Our kitchen is so small anyway, it's one of those galley kitchens from the 70s with the original yellow linoleum floor. I've been wanting to rip that floor up for years. I saw this beautiful slate tile at the home improvement store last weekend when I was looking for a new showerhead. The one we have now just trickles out water, it's so frustrating when you're trying to rinse the shampoo out of your hair. I use this special shampoo for color-treated hair, because my grays are just coming in so fast lately. I tried dyeing it myself out of a box a few months ago and it turned out this awful brassy orange color, I had to wear a hat for a week until I could get an appointment at the salon...\n\nSo, no, I don't really have a time where I feel awake. I'm just tired all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself falling asleep unintentionally during the day, such as while sitting or talking to someone?\n\nPatient: While talking to someone? Oh, no, I don't think I've ever done that. I mean, it would be pretty rude, wouldn't it? Though, to be honest, sometimes people talk so much you kind of wish you could just close your eyes and tune them out. Like my sister-in-law, Brenda. She called me last night and just went on and on for almost an hour about her new dog. It's a golden retriever mix she got from a rescue down in Springfield. Springfield is about a two-hour drive from here, and the traffic on the interstate is just horrendous this time of year with all the construction. They've been working on that same overpass since last summer, and they always close down the left lane right when everyone is trying to get home from work. My husband got a flat tire in that construction zone a few months ago because of a loose nail. We had to wait for the tow truck for over an hour in the freezing cold. He was so mad, because he had just bought those tires at Costco. Costco is great for things like that, though I usually just go there for the bulk paper towels and those big tubs of mixed nuts. They have the best macadamia nuts, I like to bake them into white chocolate chip cookies when the holidays roll around... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, falling asleep. No, I don't just fall asleep in the middle of a conversation or sitting around during the day. Just that nodding off on the couch after my shift that I mentioned earlier.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself typically waking up at the same time every day, even on weekends or days off?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I don't have a set time or anything. It kind of just depends on the day. On my days off, I try to sleep in a little, but the garbage trucks always come by so early on Saturdays. They make this awful screeching noise when they lift the bins. Our neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out there in her bathrobe once to yell at them because they dropped some trash on her driveway. She's very particular about her driveway. She just had it repaved last summer by this company that her son works for. He’s a nice boy, but he drives this massive pickup truck that’s so loud, it rattles our front windows when he comes to visit her. I’ve been meaning to get those windows replaced anyway, they let in such a terrible draft in the winter. We put that plastic shrink-wrap stuff over them last November, and it took me forever to get the tape residue off the wood frames in the spring. I had to use almost a whole bottle of Goo Gone. That stuff smells exactly like oranges, doesn't it? It reminds me of the orange marmalade my grandmother used to make. She'd put it in these little glass jars with the checkered lids and give them out for Christmas... \n\nWait, what were you asking? Oh, waking up. Yeah, no, it's never the exact same time. I just wake up whenever.\n\nDoctor: Do you work non-traditional hours or have a rotating shift schedule that could be affecting your sleep pattern?\n\nPatient: No, my hours are pretty normal. I'm a home health aide, so I just work regular daytime shifts mostly. Right now I'm mainly with this one client, Mrs. Higgins. She's a dear, really, but she keeps her thermostat set to 78 degrees year-round. It is just stifling in there. And she has these two massive Maine Coon cats that just leave hair on absolutely everything. I have to keep one of those sticky lint rollers in my car just so I don't walk into the grocery store looking like a giant furball. \n\nI usually stop at the Kroger on the way home to pick up something for dinner, but their produce section has been so disappointing lately. I bought a bag of apples last week and half of them were bruised right out of the bag. It's such a waste of money. And food is so expensive now! My husband and I used to go out to eat at that little Italian place down on 4th street every Friday, but they raised the price of their chicken parmesan by five dollars, so we just stopped going. We try to make it at home now, but my tomato sauce always turns out a little too acidic. My mother used to put a pinch of sugar in hers to cut the acid, but I can never get it to taste like hers did. She used to use those San Marzano tomatoes, the ones in the yellow can... \n\nAnyway, I'm sorry, what was the question? Oh, my schedule. Right. No, no night shifts or rotating hours or anything. Just the same daytime shifts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Overwhelming need to sleep? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but not like I'm going to just pass out in the middle of the day. I might feel a bit sluggish, especially after lunch. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... for a long time now, my sleep has just been completely broken. I mean, I fall into bed absolutely exhausted after work. Being a home health aide really takes it out of you, you know? Just last week, Mrs. Higgins—she's one of my regulars, sweet lady but she gets so confused—she had me reorganizing her entire kitchen pantry for two hours. She insisted all the cans had to be in alphabetical order. Anyway, I get home, I crash, but then I wake up to every little sound in the house. The pipes clanking, the neighbor's dog... and then I'm just up, way before the alarm even goes off, feeling like I never really rested at all. It's just exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems started after you began working as a home health aide?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I've been doing the aide work for much longer than that. The sleep... well, this has really been going on since my mother-in-law moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago. We had to move her in because she couldn't manage the stairs at her old place anymore, so we converted the downstairs den into a bedroom for her. \n\nMy husband insisted we repaint it first because she absolutely hates the color green—she says it reminds her of a hospital ward. So we spent an entire weekend painting it this peach color. I think the paint can called it 'Sunset Glow' or something ridiculous like that. But the smell of the paint just lingered for days, getting into all the furniture. I had to wash the living room curtains three times just to get the fumes out, and even then, every time the baseboard heater kicked on, you could still smell it. Plus she brought this old Persian cat with her, and the hair just gets into everything, no matter how much you vacuum...\n\nDoctor: Did your sleep start to get worse after your mother-in-law moved in with you?\n\nPatient: Well, I really couldn't tell you if it was exactly then that the sleep started getting so bad. It's hard to pin down the exact timeline, to be honest. But speaking of that cat, she insists on feeding it this incredibly smelly canned food. Not the normal stuff you get at the grocery store, but this special brand you have to order online. It smells like low tide at the beach, I swear. \n\nMy husband—bless him, he tries to help—he accidentally ordered the chicken flavor instead of the salmon last month, and you would have thought the world was ending. She actually made him drive to three different pet stores on a Sunday just to find the salmon one. And Sunday is his only day to just sit and watch football, which is a whole other argument in our house because he yells at the TV when the referee makes a bad call, and it gets the dog next door barking. The neighbors actually left a passive-aggressive note on our mailbox about it once. It was written on this floral stationery that smelled like cheap lavender perfume... so, anyway, I'm really not sure if my sleep got worse right at that specific time she moved in. It all just sort of blurs together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the stress of having your mother-in-law live with you is affecting your sleep?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe? It's stressful, sure, but... I don't know if that's exactly what's waking me up. But speaking of stress, you wouldn't believe what happened with her laundry last week. She has these delicate little embroidered handkerchiefs—who even uses handkerchiefs anymore, right? But she insists they have to be washed separately on the delicate cycle with this specific, unscented baby detergent. \n\nWell, the washing machine has been acting up. It makes this horrible grinding noise during the spin cycle, like metal scraping on metal. My husband said he'd look at it, but he's been so busy at the hardware store. They're doing inventory this month, and he comes home smelling like sawdust and industrial cleaner. He tracked mud all over the hallway runner on Tuesday, and I just bought that runner at a home goods store. It has this lovely geometric pattern, but mud just sinks right into the fibers. I spent an hour scrubbing it with club soda, which my grandmother always said was the best for stains... so, yeah, I'm not really sure if it's the stress of her being there that's messing with my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Is there a particular time of day or night when you most often wake up?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not really one specific time. My sleep has just been completely broken for a long time now. I fall into bed absolutely exhausted, but then I wake up to every single little sound in the house. \n\nLike the ice maker in the fridge, for instance. We got this new refrigerator last Black Friday because the old one kept leaking water all over the kitchen linoleum. And let me tell you, getting someone to come out and fix linoleum is impossible these days. The handyman wanted three hundred dollars just to look at it! Can you believe that? My neighbor, Susan, she had her whole kitchen redone a few years ago for less than what these people charge for a patch job now. She went with this gorgeous subway tile, though I told her white grout is an absolute nightmare to keep clean, especially if you cook a lot of pasta sauce, which she does because her husband is Italian and his mother gave her all these old family recipes... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the sleep. Every little sound wakes me up. And then I'm just lying there, and I end up wide awake way before my alarm even goes off, feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you typically wake up feeling rested before the noises wake you up, or are you waking up from a light sleep already?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't think I'm ever really in a deep sleep to begin with, honestly. Like I was saying, it's just broken. I fall into bed absolutely exhausted after being on my feet all day at work. Being a home health aide is no joke on the lower back, let me tell you. My client, Mr. Henderson, he's a sweetheart, but he refuses to use his walker when he goes to the bathroom. He insists he can hold onto the walls, but he's already fallen twice this year. \n\nThe last time he fell, the EMTs had to come, and one of them was this young kid who looked like he was barely out of high school. He had this little mustache he was trying to grow, you know the kind? It just looks like dirt on their upper lip. My nephew tried to grow one of those when he went off to college, and my sister was absolutely mortified when he came home for Thanksgiving looking like a 1970s game show host. She made him shave it right there in the downstairs half-bath before she'd even serve the turkey. And she makes a beautiful turkey, always brines it for two days with apple cider and peppercorns... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm just in this light sleep, and every little creak in the house wakes me up, and then I'm just lying there until way before the alarm goes off, and I just never feel rested.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely tired or sleepy during the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, constantly. It's like I'm dragging myself through molasses all day. By the time I finish seeing my home-health clients—and Mr. Henderson is just one of them, I also have Mrs. Gable who lives in this third-floor walk-up, and let me tell you, carrying her groceries up those stairs is a workout. She always wants these specific canned peaches, the ones in heavy syrup, not the juice. I told her the juice is better for her blood sugar, but she says she's 82 and she'll eat what she wants. Which, I guess, fair enough, right? My aunt Edna was the same way with her peppermint candies. She kept them in this little crystal dish on the coffee table, the kind that looks like a swan. I always loved that dish. I think my cousin Barbara ended up with it after the estate sale, which is typical Barbara, she always swooped in and took the best things... \n\nBut anyway, by the time I get back to my own house to help out, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing the simplest things, like charting my notes for the agency or just folding a basket of laundry, it feels like it's just... too much to push through. I'm just so drained.\n\nDoctor: Is your mother-in-law's presence the main thing you associate with your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say she's the only reason I don't sleep, but it definitely all started when she moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago now. We had to convert the den into a bedroom for her, which was a whole ordeal because my husband had all his woodworking tools in there. He makes these beautiful birdhouses, you know? Really intricate ones that look like little Victorian mansions. He sells them at the craft fair down at the Methodist church every spring. The pastor's wife bought one last year and painted it this awful neon pink color. I mean, who paints a Victorian birdhouse neon pink? It completely ruined the natural grain of the cedar. Cedar is so expensive right now, too, with all the supply chain issues. We went to Home Depot the other day and a single piece of plywood was almost double what it used to be. The guy working in the lumber aisle said it's because of the shipping containers getting stuck... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, my mother-in-law. Yeah, it's been about two years since she moved in, and that's exactly when my sleep just went completely out the window.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a regular sleep schedule?\n\nPatient: I try to, but it's really hard with my job. I usually try to get to bed around 10:30, but it really depends on when my husband gets home from his second job. He works security at the mall on the weekends and a couple of weeknights. It's the mall over on Route 9, the one that used to have that huge Sears? They tore it down and put in a giant gym with one of those indoor pools. I don't know who has time to go swimming in the middle of the day, honestly. My friend Linda goes to water aerobics there on Tuesdays. She says it's great for her knees, but she always smells like chlorine for two days afterward. I can't stand the smell of chlorine, it reminds me of when my kids were little and we'd go to the municipal pool, and the lifeguards were always blowing their whistles at everything... \n\nSo, yeah, I don't really have a set time, I guess. It just depends on the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up with a start or a sense of panic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a panic, really, but my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. I'll fall into bed completely exhausted, but then I just wake up to every little sound in the house. Like the pipes clanking. My husband tried to fix them last month with this antique pipe wrench he found at a garage sale over on Elm Street. The guy running the sale had all these vintage tools, and this huge box of old vinyl records. I used to love listening to records when I was taking a few classes at the community college, I had this Fleetwood Mac album I played until it literally wore out. You just don't get that same warm sound with digital music nowadays, you know? My kids just stream everything on their phones with those little wireless earbuds. I tried a pair once and they just kept falling right out of my ears, I don't understand how people run in those things without losing them down a storm drain... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I hear every creak and groan of the house, and then I'm just awake way before my alarm even goes off. I always get up feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually take naps during the day to try and feel more rested?\n\nPatient: I try not to, but sometimes I just can't help it. I'll sit down on the couch after I finish my shift—I work as a home health aide, mostly with elderly folks in the county. Some of them are just so sweet, like Mr. Henderson. He always offers me hard hard candies from this little crystal dish he keeps on his coffee table. It's one of those heavy, cut-glass bowls that you just don't see anymore. He said it belonged to his grandmother, who brought it over from Ireland. I've always wanted to go to Ireland, actually. I saw this documentary on PBS once about the Cliffs of Moher, and the green is just so vibrant, it almost looks fake. I tried to grow some shamrocks in a pot on my windowsill for St. Patrick's Day a few years ago, but my cat, Mittens—she's a tabby, a real menace sometimes—she just chewed them all down to the dirt. She does that to all my plants. I had to put my spider plant in a hanging basket just to keep it safe from her...\n\nBut, um, naps. Right. I don't really take them on purpose. I just kind of nod off on the couch sometimes, but it doesn't really help. I still feel just as tired when I wake up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling most awake and alert at the same time every day, regardless of when you go to bed or wake up?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. Honestly, I don't think I ever feel fully awake or alert, no matter what time it is. I'm just always kind of dragging. I try drinking coffee, but I can only have one cup in the morning or my stomach gets all upset. My husband bought me one of those fancy espresso machines for my birthday last year, the kind with the little pods? It takes up half the counter space in our kitchen. Our kitchen is so small anyway, it's one of those galley kitchens from the 70s with the original yellow linoleum floor. I've been wanting to rip that floor up for years. I saw this beautiful slate tile at the home improvement store last weekend when I was looking for a new showerhead. The one we have now just trickles out water, it's so frustrating when you're trying to rinse the shampoo out of your hair. I use this special shampoo for color-treated hair, because my grays are just coming in so fast lately. I tried dyeing it myself out of a box a few months ago and it turned out this awful brassy orange color, I had to wear a hat for a week until I could get an appointment at the salon...\n\nSo, no, I don't really have a time where I feel awake. I'm just tired all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself falling asleep unintentionally during the day, such as while sitting or talking to someone?\n\nPatient: While talking to someone? Oh, no, I don't think I've ever done that. I mean, it would be pretty rude, wouldn't it? Though, to be honest, sometimes people talk so much you kind of wish you could just close your eyes and tune them out. Like my sister-in-law, Brenda. She called me last night and just went on and on for almost an hour about her new dog. It's a golden retriever mix she got from a rescue down in Springfield. Springfield is about a two-hour drive from here, and the traffic on the interstate is just horrendous this time of year with all the construction. They've been working on that same overpass since last summer, and they always close down the left lane right when everyone is trying to get home from work. My husband got a flat tire in that construction zone a few months ago because of a loose nail. We had to wait for the tow truck for over an hour in the freezing cold. He was so mad, because he had just bought those tires at Costco. Costco is great for things like that, though I usually just go there for the bulk paper towels and those big tubs of mixed nuts. They have the best macadamia nuts, I like to bake them into white chocolate chip cookies when the holidays roll around... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, falling asleep. No, I don't just fall asleep in the middle of a conversation or sitting around during the day. Just that nodding off on the couch after my shift that I mentioned earlier.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself typically waking up at the same time every day, even on weekends or days off?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I don't have a set time or anything. It kind of just depends on the day. On my days off, I try to sleep in a little, but the garbage trucks always come by so early on Saturdays. They make this awful screeching noise when they lift the bins. Our neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out there in her bathrobe once to yell at them because they dropped some trash on her driveway. She's very particular about her driveway. She just had it repaved last summer by this company that her son works for. He’s a nice boy, but he drives this massive pickup truck that’s so loud, it rattles our front windows when he comes to visit her. I’ve been meaning to get those windows replaced anyway, they let in such a terrible draft in the winter. We put that plastic shrink-wrap stuff over them last November, and it took me forever to get the tape residue off the wood frames in the spring. I had to use almost a whole bottle of Goo Gone. That stuff smells exactly like oranges, doesn't it? It reminds me of the orange marmalade my grandmother used to make. She'd put it in these little glass jars with the checkered lids and give them out for Christmas... \n\nWait, what were you asking? Oh, waking up. Yeah, no, it's never the exact same time. I just wake up whenever.\n\nDoctor: Do you work non-traditional hours or have a rotating shift schedule that could be affecting your sleep pattern?\n\nPatient: No, my hours are pretty normal. I'm a home health aide, so I just work regular daytime shifts mostly. Right now I'm mainly with this one client, Mrs. Higgins. She's a dear, really, but she keeps her thermostat set to 78 degrees year-round. It is just stifling in there. And she has these two massive Maine Coon cats that just leave hair on absolutely everything. I have to keep one of those sticky lint rollers in my car just so I don't walk into the grocery store looking like a giant furball. \n\nI usually stop at the Kroger on the way home to pick up something for dinner, but their produce section has been so disappointing lately. I bought a bag of apples last week and half of them were bruised right out of the bag. It's such a waste of money. And food is so expensive now! My husband and I used to go out to eat at that little Italian place down on 4th street every Friday, but they raised the price of their chicken parmesan by five dollars, so we just stopped going. We try to make it at home now, but my tomato sauce always turns out a little too acidic. My mother used to put a pinch of sugar in hers to cut the acid, but I can never get it to taste like hers did. She used to use those San Marzano tomatoes, the ones in the yellow can... \n\nAnyway, I'm sorry, what was the question? Oh, my schedule. Right. No, no night shifts or rotating hours or anything. Just the same daytime shifts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel an overwhelming need to sleep during the day, even after a full night's rest?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming need to sleep? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but not like I'm going to just pass out in the middle of the day. I might feel a bit sluggish, especially after lunch. I usually pack a sandwich, turkey and swiss on whole wheat, but sometimes the bread gets a little soggy by the time noon rolls around. I tried putting the condiments in a little separate container, you know, those tiny plastic ones you get for salad dressing? But I always end up losing the lids. We had to run two cycles with that special cleaner just to get the smell out. The cleaner was supposed to smell like lemon, but it smelled more like... Just the regular kind of tired.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Fall asleep at any moment? No, not exactly like that. It's more like... well, by the time I finish seeing my home-health clients and come back to help out at home, my body just feels incredibly heavy. Like lead. Even just finishing up my charting notes or trying to fold a basket of laundry can feel like way too much to push through. 

Honestly, the laundry just piles up on the chair in the bedroom sometimes. I actually just had to switch detergents recently because my husband was complaining that the lavender one was making his nose itch. We bought that giant tub of the powder kind from Costco, you know the one? It's so heavy I could barely lift it into the cart. Costco is always such a madhouse anyway, especially on the weekends. I went last Tuesday thinking it would be quieter, but there was a line of carts blocking the aisle just to get a free sample of those little pizza bagels. They used to give you a whole half of a bagel, but now they cut them into these tiny little quarters. It’s hardly even worth stopping for. But my husband loves them, so I ended up buying a box. They take up half the freezer! We had to take the ice maker bin out just to make them fit, which is a pain because now we have to use those old plastic ice cube trays. I always spill water all over the floor trying to walk them from the sink to the freezer... 

I'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Falling asleep? Right, no, I don't just fall asleep out of nowhere. It's just that heavy, dragging exhaustion when I'm trying to get my notes or the chores done at the end of the day.

Like I was saying, it's just broken. I fall into bed absolutely exhausted after being on my feet all day at work. Being a home health aide is no joke on the lower back, let me tell you. It's like I'm dragging myself through molasses all day. Which, I guess, fair enough, right? My aunt Edna was the same way with her peppermint candies. She kept them in this little crystal dish on the coffee table, the kind that looks like a swan. I always loved that dish. I'm just so drained.\n\nDoctor: Is your mother-in-law's presence the main thing you associate with your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say she's the only reason I don't sleep, but it definitely all started when she moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago now. We had to convert the den into a bedroom for her, which was a whole ordeal because my husband had all his woodworking tools in there. He makes these beautiful birdhouses, you know? Really intricate ones that look like little Victorian mansions. He sells them at the craft fair down at the Methodist church every spring. The pastor's wife bought one last year and painted it this awful neon pink color. I mean, who paints a Victorian birdhouse neon pink? It completely ruined the natural grain of the cedar. Cedar is so expensive right now, too, with all the supply chain issues. We went to Home Depot the other day and a single piece of plywood was almost double what it used to be. The guy working in the lumber aisle said it's because of the shipping containers getting stuck... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, my mother-in-law. Yeah, it's been about two years since she moved in, and that's exactly when my sleep just went completely out the window.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a regular sleep schedule?\n\nPatient: I try to, but it's really hard with my job. I usually try to get to bed around 10:30, but it really depends on when my husband gets home from his second job. He works security at the mall on the weekends and a couple of weeknights. It's the mall over on Route 9, the one that used to have that huge Sears? They tore it down and put in a giant gym with one of those indoor pools. I don't know who has time to go swimming in the middle of the day, honestly. My friend Linda goes to water aerobics there on Tuesdays. She says it's great for her knees, but she always smells like chlorine for two days afterward. I can't stand the smell of chlorine, it reminds me of when my kids were little and we'd go to the municipal pool, and the lifeguards were always blowing their whistles at everything... \n\nSo, yeah, I don't really have a set time, I guess. It just depends on the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up with a start or a sense of panic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a panic, really, but my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. My husband tried to fix them last month with this antique pipe wrench he found at a garage sale over on Elm Street. The guy running the sale had all these vintage tools, and this huge box of old vinyl records. I used to love listening to records when I was taking a few classes at the community college, I had this Fleetwood Mac album I played until it literally wore out. You just don't get that same warm sound with digital music nowadays, you know? My kids just stream everything on their phones with those little wireless earbuds. I tried a pair once and they just kept falling right out of my ears, I don't understand how people run in those things without losing them down a storm drain... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I hear every creak and groan of the house, and then I'm just awake way before my alarm even goes off. I always get up feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually take naps during the day to try and feel more rested?\n\nPatient: I try not to, but sometimes I just can't help it. I just kind of nod off on the couch sometimes, but it doesn't really help. I still feel just as tired when I wake up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling most awake and alert at the same time every day, regardless of when you go to bed or wake up?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. Honestly, I don't think I ever feel fully awake or alert, no matter what time it is. I'm just always kind of dragging. I try drinking coffee, but I can only have one cup in the morning or my stomach gets all upset. My husband bought me one of those fancy espresso machines for my birthday last year, the kind with the little pods? It takes up half the counter space in our kitchen. Our kitchen is so small anyway, it's one of those galley kitchens from the 70s with the original yellow linoleum floor. I've been wanting to rip that floor up for years. I saw this beautiful slate tile at the home improvement store last weekend when I was looking for a new showerhead. The one we have now just trickles out water, it's so frustrating when you're trying to rinse the shampoo out of your hair. I use this special shampoo for color-treated hair, because my grays are just coming in so fast lately. I tried dyeing it myself out of a box a few months ago and it turned out this awful brassy orange color, I had to wear a hat for a week until I could get an appointment at the salon...\n\nSo, no, I don't really have a time where I feel awake. I'm just tired all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself falling asleep unintentionally during the day, such as while sitting or talking to someone?\n\nPatient: While talking to someone? Oh, no, I don't think I've ever done that. I mean, it would be pretty rude, wouldn't it? They have the best macadamia nuts, I like to bake them into white chocolate chip cookies when the holidays roll around... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, falling asleep. No, I don't just fall asleep in the middle of a conversation or sitting around during the day. They make this awful screeching noise when they lift the bins. She just had it repaved last summer by this company that her son works for. He’s a nice boy, but he drives this massive pickup truck that’s so loud, it rattles our front windows when he comes to visit her. I just wake up whenever.\n\nDoctor: Do you work non-traditional hours or have a rotating shift schedule that could be affecting your sleep pattern?\n\nPatient: No, my hours are pretty normal. I'm a home health aide, so I just work regular daytime shifts mostly. Right now I'm mainly with this one client, Mrs. Higgins. I bought a bag of apples last week and half of them were bruised right out of the bag. It's such a waste of money. And food is so expensive now! My husband and I used to go out to eat at that little Italian place down on 4th street every Friday, but they raised the price of their chicken parmesan by five dollars, so we just stopped going. Most rested or alert? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I don't really have a time where I feel like I'm suddenly wide awake and ready to go, if that's what you mean. Cedar is so expensive right now, too, with all the supply chain issues. We went to Home Depot the other day and a single piece of plywood was almost double what it used to be. The guy working in the lumber aisle said it's because of the shipping containers getting stuck... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, my mother-in-law. I usually try to get to bed around 10:30, but it really depends on when my husband gets home from his second job. He works security at the mall on the weekends and a couple of weeknights. I don't know who has time to go swimming in the middle of the day, honestly. My friend Linda goes to water aerobics there on Tuesdays. She says it's great for her knees, but she always smells like chlorine for two days afterward. I can't stand the smell of chlorine, it reminds me of when my kids were little and we'd go to the municipal pool, and the lifeguards were always blowing their whistles at everything... \n\nSo, yeah, I don't really have a set time, I guess. A different internal clock? Um, no, I don't think so. I've always been pretty normal with that kind of thing. Up in the morning, sleep at night. Well, try to sleep at night anyway. It's not like my body wants to be awake at 3 AM. Although, my neighbor's dog sure wants to be awake at 3 AM. It's a beagle, I think? Or maybe a basset hound mix. The texture is all wrong. It's crunchy! Spaghetti shouldn't be crunchy...

Wait, what were we talking about? Internal clocks? Right, no, I don't think my clock is backwards or anything like that. I just feel exhausted all day.

He had this little mustache he was trying to grow, you know the kind? It just looks like dirt on their upper lip. She made him shave it right there in the downstairs half-bath before she'd even serve the turkey. It's like I'm dragging myself through molasses all day. By the time I finish seeing my home-health clients—and Mr. Henderson is just one of them, I also have Mrs. Gable who lives in this third-floor walk-up, and let me tell you, carrying her groceries up those stairs is a workout. She always wants these specific canned peaches, the ones in heavy syrup, not the juice. I told her the juice is better for her blood sugar, but she says she's 82 and she'll eat what she wants. Which, I guess, fair enough, right? My aunt Edna was the same way with her peppermint candies. She kept them in this little crystal dish on the coffee table, the kind that looks like a swan. I always loved that dish. I think my cousin Barbara ended up with it after the estate sale, which is typical Barbara, she always swooped in and took the best things... \n\nBut anyway, by the time I get back to my own house to help out, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing the simplest things, like charting my notes for the agency or just folding a basket of laundry, it feels like it's just... too much to push through. I'm just so drained.\n\nDoctor: Is your mother-in-law's presence the main thing you associate with your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say she's the only reason I don't sleep, but it definitely all started when she moved in with us. That was a little over two years ago now. We had to convert the den into a bedroom for her, which was a whole ordeal because my husband had all his woodworking tools in there. He makes these beautiful birdhouses, you know? Really intricate ones that look like little Victorian mansions. He sells them at the craft fair down at the Methodist church every spring. The pastor's wife bought one last year and painted it this awful neon pink color. I mean, who paints a Victorian birdhouse neon pink? It completely ruined the natural grain of the cedar. Cedar is so expensive right now, too, with all the supply chain issues. We went to Home Depot the other day and a single piece of plywood was almost double what it used to be. The guy working in the lumber aisle said it's because of the shipping containers getting stuck... \n\nBut anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, my mother-in-law. Yeah, it's been about two years since she moved in, and that's exactly when my sleep just went completely out the window.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a regular sleep schedule?\n\nPatient: I try to, but it's really hard with my job. I usually try to get to bed around 10:30, but it really depends on when my husband gets home from his second job. He works security at the mall on the weekends and a couple of weeknights. It's the mall over on Route 9, the one that used to have that huge Sears? They tore it down and put in a giant gym with one of those indoor pools. I don't know who has time to go swimming in the middle of the day, honestly. My friend Linda goes to water aerobics there on Tuesdays. She says it's great for her knees, but she always smells like chlorine for two days afterward. I can't stand the smell of chlorine, it reminds me of when my kids were little and we'd go to the municipal pool, and the lifeguards were always blowing their whistles at everything... \n\nSo, yeah, I don't really have a set time, I guess. It just depends on the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up with a start or a sense of panic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd call it a panic, really, but my sleep has just been so broken for a long time now. I'll fall into bed completely exhausted, but then I just wake up to every little sound in the house. Like the pipes clanking. My husband tried to fix them last month with this antique pipe wrench he found at a garage sale over on Elm Street. The guy running the sale had all these vintage tools, and this huge box of old vinyl records. I used to love listening to records when I was taking a few classes at the community college, I had this Fleetwood Mac album I played until it literally wore out. You just don't get that same warm sound with digital music nowadays, you know? My kids just stream everything on their phones with those little wireless earbuds. I tried a pair once and they just kept falling right out of my ears, I don't understand how people run in those things without losing them down a storm drain... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I hear every creak and groan of the house, and then I'm just awake way before my alarm even goes off. I always get up feeling like I never really rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually take naps during the day to try and feel more rested?\n\nPatient: I try not to, but sometimes I just can't help it. I'll sit down on the couch after I finish my shift—I work as a home health aide, mostly with elderly folks in the county. Some of them are just so sweet, like Mr. Henderson. He always offers me hard hard candies from this little crystal dish he keeps on his coffee table. It's one of those heavy, cut-glass bowls that you just don't see anymore. He said it belonged to his grandmother, who brought it over from Ireland. I've always wanted to go to Ireland, actually. I saw this documentary on PBS once about the Cliffs of Moher, and the green is just so vibrant, it almost looks fake. I tried to grow some shamrocks in a pot on my windowsill for St. Patrick's Day a few years ago, but my cat, Mittens—she's a tabby, a real menace sometimes—she just chewed them all down to the dirt. She does that to all my plants. I had to put my spider plant in a hanging basket just to keep it safe from her...\n\nBut, um, naps. Right. I don't really take them on purpose. I just kind of nod off on the couch sometimes, but it doesn't really help. I still feel just as tired when I wake up.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling most awake and alert at the same time every day, regardless of when you go to bed or wake up?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. Honestly, I don't think I ever feel fully awake or alert, no matter what time it is. I'm just always kind of dragging. I try drinking coffee, but I can only have one cup in the morning or my stomach gets all upset. My husband bought me one of those fancy espresso machines for my birthday last year, the kind with the little pods? It takes up half the counter space in our kitchen. Our kitchen is so small anyway, it's one of those galley kitchens from the 70s with the original yellow linoleum floor. I've been wanting to rip that floor up for years. I saw this beautiful slate tile at the home improvement store last weekend when I was looking for a new showerhead. The one we have now just trickles out water, it's so frustrating when you're trying to rinse the shampoo out of your hair. I use this special shampoo for color-treated hair, because my grays are just coming in so fast lately. I tried dyeing it myself out of a box a few months ago and it turned out this awful brassy orange color, I had to wear a hat for a week until I could get an appointment at the salon...\n\nSo, no, I don't really have a time where I feel awake. I'm just tired all day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself falling asleep unintentionally during the day, such as while sitting or talking to someone?\n\nPatient: While talking to someone? Oh, no, I don't think I've ever done that. I mean, it would be pretty rude, wouldn't it? She called me last night and just went on and on for almost an hour about her new dog. It's a golden retriever mix she got from a rescue down in Springfield. Springfield is about a two-hour drive from here, and the traffic on the interstate is just horrendous this time of year with all the construction. They've been working on that same overpass since last summer, and they always close down the left lane right when everyone is trying to get home from work. My husband got a flat tire in that construction zone a few months ago because of a loose nail. We had to wait for the tow truck for over an hour in the freezing cold. He was so mad, because he had just bought those tires at Costco. Costco is great for things like that, though I usually just go there for the bulk paper towels and those big tubs of mixed nuts. No, I don't just fall asleep in the middle of a conversation or sitting around during the day. Just that nodding off on the couch after my shift that I mentioned earlier.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself typically waking up at the same time every day, even on weekends or days off?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I don't have a set time or anything. It kind of just depends on the day. On my days off, I try to sleep in a little, but the garbage trucks always come by so early on Saturdays. They make this awful screeching noise when they lift the bins. Our neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out there in her bathrobe once to yell at them because they dropped some trash on her driveway. She's very particular about her driveway. She just had it repaved last summer by this company that her son works for. He’s a nice boy, but he drives this massive pickup truck that’s so loud, it rattles our front windows when he comes to visit her. I’ve been meaning to get those windows replaced anyway, they let in such a terrible draft in the winter. We put that plastic shrink-wrap stuff over them last November, and it took me forever to get the tape residue off the wood frames in the spring. I had to use almost a whole bottle of Goo Gone. That stuff smells exactly like oranges, doesn't it? It reminds me of the orange marmalade my grandmother used to make. She'd put it in these little glass jars with the checkered lids and give them out for Christmas... \n\nWait, what were you asking? Oh, waking up. Yeah, no, it's never the exact same time. I just wake up whenever.\n\nDoctor: Do you work non-traditional hours or have a rotating shift schedule that could be affecting your sleep pattern?\n\nPatient: No, my hours are pretty normal. I'm a home health aide, so I just work regular daytime shifts mostly. Right now I'm mainly with this one client, Mrs. Higgins. I bought a bag of apples last week and half of them were bruised right out of the bag. It's such a waste of money. And food is so expensive now! My husband and I used to go out to eat at that little Italian place down on 4th street every Friday, but they raised the price of their chicken parmesan by five dollars, so we just stopped going. We try to make it at home now, but my tomato sauce always turns out a little too acidic. My mother used to put a pinch of sugar in hers to cut the acid, but I can never get it to taste like hers did. She used to use those San Marzano tomatoes, the ones in the yellow can... \n\nAnyway, I'm sorry, what was the question? Oh, my schedule. Right. No, no night shifts or rotating hours or anything. Just the same daytime shifts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel an overwhelming need to sleep during the day, even after a full night's rest?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming need to sleep? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but not like I'm going to just pass out in the middle of the day. I might feel a bit sluggish, especially after lunch. I usually pack a sandwich, turkey and swiss on whole wheat, but sometimes the bread gets a little soggy by the time noon rolls around. I tried putting the condiments in a little separate container, you know, those tiny plastic ones you get for salad dressing? But I always end up losing the lids. I'm a home health aide, so I just work regular daytime shifts mostly. Right now I'm mainly with this one client, Mrs. Higgins. She's a dear, really, but she keeps her thermostat set to 78 degrees year-round. It is just stifling in there. And she has these two massive Maine Coon cats that just leave hair on absolutely everything. I have to keep one of those sticky lint rollers in my car just so I don't walk into the grocery store looking like a giant furball. \n\nI usually stop at the Kroger on the way home to pick up something for dinner, but their produce section has been so disappointing lately. I bought a bag of apples last week and half of them were bruised right out of the bag. It's such a waste of money. And food is so expensive now! My husband and I used to go out to eat at that little Italian place down on 4th street every Friday, but they raised the price of their chicken parmesan by five dollars, so we just stopped going. We try to make it at home now, but my tomato sauce always turns out a little too acidic. My mother used to put a pinch of sugar in hers to cut the acid, but I can never get it to taste like hers did. She used to use those San Marzano tomatoes, the ones in the yellow can... \n\nAnyway, I'm sorry, what was the question? Oh, my schedule. Right. No, no night shifts or rotating hours or anything. Just the same daytime shifts.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel an overwhelming need to sleep during the day, even after a full night's rest?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming need to sleep? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, but not like I'm going to just pass out in the middle of the day. I might feel a bit sluggish, especially after lunch. I usually pack a sandwich, turkey and swiss on whole wheat, but sometimes the bread gets a little soggy by the time noon rolls around. I tried putting the condiments in a little separate container, you know, those tiny plastic ones you get for salad dressing? But I always end up losing the lids. They just vanish into the abyss of the dishwasher. I swear, that machine eats things. Last month, it swallowed a whole teaspoon. A nice silver one from the set we got for our wedding. My husband was furious, he spent an hour taking the bottom filter apart trying to find it. It was so gross down there, all covered in this gray sludge. We had to run two cycles with that special cleaner just to get the smell out. The cleaner was supposed to smell like lemon, but it smelled more like... I don't know, industrial floor wax? \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Overwhelming need to sleep? No, I don't get that. Just the regular kind of tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel so tired during the day that you could fall asleep at any moment?\n\nPatient: Fall asleep at any moment? No, not exactly like that. It's more like... well, by the time I finish seeing my home-health clients and come back to help out at home, my body just feels incredibly heavy. Like lead. Even just finishing up my charting notes or trying to fold a basket of laundry can feel like way too much to push through. \n\nHonestly, the laundry just piles up on the chair in the bedroom sometimes. I actually just had to switch detergents recently because my husband was complaining that the lavender one was making his nose itch. We bought that giant tub of the powder kind from Costco, you know the one? It's so heavy I could barely lift it into the cart. Costco is always such a madhouse anyway, especially on the weekends. I went last Tuesday thinking it would be quieter, but there was a line of carts blocking the aisle just to get a free sample of those little pizza bagels. They used to give you a whole half of a bagel, but now they cut them into these tiny little quarters. It’s hardly even worth stopping for. But my husband loves them, so I ended up buying a box. They take up half the freezer! We had to take the ice maker bin out just to make them fit, which is a pain because now we have to use those old plastic ice cube trays. I always spill water all over the floor trying to walk them from the sink to the freezer... \n\nI'm sorry, I lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Falling asleep? Right, no, I don't just fall asleep out of nowhere. Um... I don't think so. I mean, I don't really have a time where I feel like I'm suddenly wide awake and ready to go, if that's what you mean. It's just sort of a steady tiredness all day. My husband, though, he's a night owl. He gets this burst of energy around 10 PM and starts wanting to reorganize the garage or clean out the gutters. It drives me crazy because I'm just trying to wind down and watch my shows. We've been watching that new baking competition on channel 4, the one where they have to make everything out of chocolate? Sleep in as late as I want? Oh, I wish. No, not really. Even on the days I don't have to go see my clients for the agency, there's always something that gets me up. Usually my daughter drops the grandkids off on Saturdays so she can pick up an extra shift at the diner. She works at that diner on Route 9, you know the one with the giant neon coffee cup that flashes? It’s been there forever. I actually used to go there when I was in high school. We’d get milkshakes after the football games. They used to make them with real hand-scooped ice cream, but now I’m pretty sure they just use that cheap soft-serve mix from a bag. It melts way too fast. I got a strawberry one last month and it was basically soup by the time I got to my car. And my car... oh my gosh, the backseat is still stained from where my youngest grandson squeezed his grape juice box too hard. I tried scrubbing it with that cleaner they sell on late-night TV, the one with the guy who yells all the time? It didn't work at all. It just made the purple stain spread out into a bigger circle, and now my whole car smells like wet dog and fake lemons. I even tried leaving the windows down overnight to air it out, but then it rained unexpectedly, so the seats got all damp and... 

I'm sorry, I'm rambling again, aren't I? What did you ask me? Sleeping in? Right, no. Between the grandkids and everything else around the house, I don't really have any days where I can just sleep until I'm ready to wake up.
